GF-MOTOR-005
======================================================================

Files:
    core/application/bootstrap.py
    core/application/application.py
    main.py
    ui/plugins/plugin_context.py
    plugins/motor_dynamics/plugin.py

Status:
    🟠 HOST EXTENSION COMPOSITION PATH MISSING — SOURCE TRACE CONFIRMED

Source-audit findings:
    create_application() composes one CommandManager and registers the
    built-in handler families in core/application/bootstrap.py. Its draft
    commit handler resolves the Application-owned CreationContractRegistry.

    Application exposes configure_creation_contracts() and
    creation_contract_registry, but direct inspection of main.py found no
    call configuring the registry. The registry is constructed empty by
    Application.__init__ and resolve() rejects an unconfigured registry.

    Application does not expose a verified public extension handler
    registration/unregistration API. Built-in handler registration occurs
    inside bootstrap composition, while project activation rebuilds the
    CommandManager. Registering only against the current private
    _command_manager would therefore bypass the public boundary and would
    not establish lifecycle-safe registration across project transitions.

    main.py creates PluginContext without services. The default PluginManager
    definitions also do not include motor_dynamics. Consequently the plugin's
    required motor_dynamics_composition service is not provided by startup
    composition and the plugin is not activated by the default plugin set.

Decision:
    Do not add a plugin-local workaround or mutate the private
    Application._command_manager. Before motor runtime activation, define
    one Application-owned extension composition seam that:
      1. supplies trusted motor creation metadata to the existing
         CreationContractRegistry;
      2. registers extension handlers atomically with each active
         CommandManager runtime and supports teardown;
      3. survives project/runtime replacement without duplicate handlers;
      4. injects the explicit motor composition adapter and motor service
         through PluginContext.services only after those services exist.
    Preserve the canonical DraftNetwork → CreationCommandPreparer → Core
    mutation path. Do not create a parallel creation registry or topology.

Validation:
    Direct source inspection only. No tests or CI run. No runtime behavior,
    import integrity, or project-transition activation was validated.
    PR #674 remains draft and must not be merged without explicit instruction.

======================================================================
GF-MOTOR-006
======================================================================

Files:
    core/application/bootstrap.py
    core/application/application.py
    core/application/command_manager.py
    core/application/project_lifecycle.py

Status:
    🟠 EXTENSION REGISTRATION MUST BE RUNTIME-FACTORY BASED

Source-audit findings:
    bootstrap.create_application() defines build_runtime(active_network),
    which constructs network-scoped ModelService, relay/protection handlers,
    CommandManager, ElectricalInsertionService, DraftCommandHandlers, and
    CommitNetworkHandler. Project activation calls build_runtime again for
    each candidate Network and atomically installs the new manager through
    Application._replace_runtime(); the lifecycle owns rollback callbacks.

    CommandManager exposes register_handler() and unregister_handler(), but
    those methods affect only that manager instance. Registering a fixed set
    of plugin handlers against the current manager would not register them
    on future managers created by build_runtime().

    A handler factory must be composed for every runtime generation and must
    receive the active Network/runtime dependencies. Factories must be invoked
    before the candidate runtime is published, and any partial failure must
    abort candidate runtime construction without mutating the active runtime.
    Project activation rollback must restore the prior runtime and must not
    leave duplicate extension registrations.

    The existing Application._replace_runtime() also adds SLD handlers to the
    candidate manager before publishing it. This is the closest established
    lifecycle pattern, but extension registration needs an explicit public
    contract and should not expose the private manager to plugins.

Decision:
    Do not register motor handlers directly on the live CommandManager.
    Implement a host-owned extension factory registry at the Application
    composition boundary, with stable extension IDs, duplicate command-owner
    detection, atomic candidate-runtime composition, deterministic teardown,
    and trusted creation-contract configuration. The motor plugin should
    provide a factory/adapter, not retain handlers bound to a stale Network.

Validation:
    Source inspection only. No tests or CI run. No code/runtime behavior
    was changed by this entry. PR #674 remains open, draft, and unmerged.

======================================================================

======================================================================
GF-MOTOR-007
======================================================================

Files:
    core/application/application.py
    core/application/creation.py
    core/application/bootstrap.py
    plugins/motor_dynamics/plugin.py

Status:
    🟠 CREATION-CONTRACT COMPOSITION MUST PRECEDE MOTOR ACTIVATION

Source-audit findings:
    Application.__init__ constructs an empty CreationContractRegistry.
    Application.configure_creation_contracts() delegates to its configure()
    method. CreationContractRegistry rejects resolve() while empty and refuses
    replacement with a structurally different contract set after configuration.

    Repository source search and main.py inspection found no call site that
    configures this registry on the ordinary startup path. The canonical
    CreationCommandPreparer therefore cannot prepare a DraftEquipment commit
    until the host composition supplies a complete trusted contract snapshot.

    The registry's configure() operation expects a non-empty contract mapping
    and the canonical registry is not a motor-only additive registry. Configuring
    just a motor contract later would risk omitting existing equipment contracts
    or conflicting with an already frozen full snapshot. Extension registration
    must not silently replace the trusted registry.

    bootstrap.create_application() closes over build_runtime(active_network).
    That function constructs candidate handler mappings before creating the
    candidate CommandManager; project activation invokes it again. This is the
    correct location to compose runtime-specific extension handler factories,
    but a registration API must also define how trusted creation metadata is
    merged and frozen before the first draft commit.

Decision:
    Do not implement a motor-only contract overwrite or claim that runtime
    registration alone completes motor creation. Before motor activation,
    reconcile the complete canonical creation-contract catalog and establish
    one composition-time configuration call. Then add runtime extension
    factories to candidate CommandManager construction with duplicate ownership
    checks and rollback-safe publication. The plugin must consume the resulting
    Application-owned adapter via PluginContext.services.

Validation:
    Source inspection only. No tests or CI run. No runtime changes were made
    by this entry. PR #674 remains open, draft, and unmerged.

======================================================================

    required by CreationContractRegistry.

    bootstrap.create_application() builds runtime-specific handler mappings

    project/network activation. The inspected ordinary composition path does
    not configure Application.creation_contract_registry.

    main.py constructs the Application and initializes the default plugin
    manager. The inspected PluginContext construction does not provide a
    motor_dynamics_composition service. The extension therefore cannot be
    treated as runtime-activatable based on the existing plugin branch alone.

    the current CommandManager. Continue direct inspection to locate the
    authoritative full equipment creation metadata and establish one trusted
    composition-time catalogue. Then implement Application-owned extension
    factories that compose against each candidate runtime, reject duplicate
    command ownership, and abort candidate publication on partial failure.
    Supply the motor plugin its composition adapter through PluginContext
    services only after that host contract exists.

    ui/creation/creation_definition.py provides declarative equipment
    CreationDefinition values with engineering parameter definitions,
    command type and ID field, parameter mapping, endpoint mapping,
    terminal requirements, topology requirements, and conditional rules.

    schema. Establish one dependency-safe catalogue/contract representation

GF-MOTOR-010
======================================================================

Files:
    ui/creation/creation_definition.py
    ui/equipment/equipment_definition.py
    ui/equipment/equipment_registry.py
    core/application/creation.py

Status:
    🟠 CREATION SCHEMA AUTHORITY IS IN THE WRONG LAYER

Source-audit findings:
    ui/creation/creation_definition.py explicitly describes itself as the
    single creation-schema authority, but it imports
    EngineeringParameterDefinition from ui/equipment/equipment_definition.py.
    That makes the complete creation schema transitively UI-owned.

    ui/equipment/equipment_registry.py builds its catalogue by calling
    creation_definition_for() and passes the result into UI EquipmentDefinition.
    core/application/creation.py independently owns the trusted commit-time
    CreationContractRegistry and command class mapping. There is no
    dependency-safe canonical contract source shared by both layers.

    This is not solved by configuring Application from main.py using a UI
    EquipmentRegistry instance: doing so would make Application trust a
    UI-owned object and could allow presentation/plugin registration to
    become authoritative engineering schema.

Decision:
    Reconcile the shared schema types and built-in catalogue into a
    framework-neutral Application/domain contract module with no UI imports.
    Keep UI EquipmentDefinition and EquipmentRegistry as presentation/tool
    adapters over that shared catalogue. Application composition should
    configure CreationContractRegistry from the trusted framework-neutral
    catalogue before draft commit. Extension contracts require explicit
    Application-owned registration/validation and must not mutate the frozen
    built-in snapshot implicitly.

Validation:
    Direct source inspection only. No tests or CI run. No runtime code
    changed by this entry. PR #674 remains open, draft, and unmerged.

GF-MOTOR-011
======================================================================

Files:
    ui/creation/creation_definition.py
    core/application/creation.py
    core/application/draft/handlers.py

Status:
    🟠 CONTRACT CONVERSION SEMANTICS MUST BE PRESERVED

Source-audit findings:
    CreationDefinition has distinct terminal_requirements and
    topology_requirements. Its endpoint_mapping maps semantic endpoint roles
    to immutable create-command fields. The Application CreationContractRegistry
    separately validates terminal_contract, endpoint_mapping, and
    required_connection_terminals.

    A conversion must not collapse these concepts. In particular, a model's
    complete terminal inventory is not identical to its required topology
    connections. Measurement-device secondary terminals are model terminals,
    while primary electrical endpoints are the relevant SimpleWire/draft
    topology endpoints. Required connection cardinality remains a commit-time
    policy and must be derived from explicit topology requirements, not inferred
    merely because a terminal exists or has an endpoint field.

    The current registry's endpoint mapping validation requires mapped role
    keys to be present in terminal_contract. The converter must validate this
    invariant against the full catalogue before any registry configuration.
    It must also preserve parameter defaults, units, choices, numeric bounds,
    validation rules, and conditional requirements when converting parameter
    definitions to the immutable Application contract representation.

Decision:
    Implement a framework-neutral schema/catalogue source and an explicit,
    deterministic conversion to CreationContractRegistry's contract shape.
    Reconcile all built-in equipment types and command signatures before
    wiring configuration into startup. Reject incomplete or conflicting
    contracts at composition rather than silently weakening commit validation.

Validation:
    Source inspection only. No tests or CI run. No runtime code changed by
    this entry. PR #674 remains open, draft, and unmerged.

======================================================================

GF-MOTOR-013
======================================================================

Files:
    ui/creation/creation_definition.py

Status:
    🟡 SOURCE CORRECTION COMMITTED; RUNTIME COMPATIBILITY NOT EXECUTED

Finding:
    CreationDefinition is declared as a frozen dataclass and described as an
    immutable creation contract, but its parameter_mapping and endpoint_mapping
    were copied into ordinary mutable dictionaries in __post_init__. Frozen
    dataclasses prevent field reassignment but do not freeze nested mappings;
    callers could therefore mutate a registered definition after validation.

Correction:
    Wrap both normalized mapping values in types.MappingProxyType. The mapping
    contents remain readable and iterable by the existing consumers but cannot
    be modified through the stored contract object.

Validation:
    Source diff only. No tests, CI, or runtime compatibility checks were run.
    Serialization/copy compatibility remains to be checked as part of the
    framework-neutral catalogue migration. PR #674 remains open, draft, and
    unmerged.

    still could not obtain trusted built-in definitions without a UI-layer
    dependency.

Correction:
    Moved the catalogue helper functions and complete built-in
    creation_definition_for() catalogue into core.application.creation_schema.
    The UI creation module now imports and re-exports the neutral catalogue
    function and shared schema types, preserving existing import names. No
    Application startup wiring or registry conversion is introduced by this
    change.

Validation:
    Source transformation and static ownership review only. No tests, CI,
    syntax compilation, or runtime imports were executed. Complete catalogue
    reconciliation against Application command signatures and registry shape
    remains pending. PR #674 remains open, draft, and unmerged.

GF-MOTOR-016
======================================================================

Files:
    ui/equipment/equipment_definition.py

Status:
    🟡 SERIALIZED CREATION CONTRACT ROUND-TRIP PRESERVED IN SOURCE

Finding:
    EquipmentDefinition.to_dict() serialized only a subset of CreationDefinition
    fields, omitting command_type, parameter definitions, terminal/topology
    requirements, conditional rules, and creation behavior flags. from_dict()
    then ignored the serialized mappings and silently rebuilt a built-in
    contract using creation_definition_for(). Custom or changed contracts could
    therefore lose schema information during a serialization round trip.

Correction:
    Serialize the complete CreationDefinition contract and reconstruct it from
    the serialized fields, including immutable schema parameter metadata,
    terminal and topology requirements, conditional requirements, and behavior
    flags. The deserializer no longer substitutes a built-in catalogue entry
    for a serialized contract. Older partial creation_definition payloads that
    lack required identity fields now fail explicitly instead of silently
    changing contract meaning.

Validation:
    Source-level edit and target replacement reviewed only. No tests, CI, or
    runtime imports were executed. Serialization compatibility remains to be
    checked during the wider framework-neutral catalogue migration. PR #674
    remains open, draft, and unmerged.

Finding:
    CreationContractRegistry validated that declared mappings targeted real
    constructor fields, but did not reject a contract that omitted a required
    constructor argument entirely. Such a contract could pass configuration
    and fail later when CreationCommandPreparer invoked the command constructor.

    canonical command signature and requires each to be sourced by the object
    ID, parameter mapping, or endpoint mapping. The registry rejects missing

GF-MOTOR-022
======================================================================

Files:
    core/application/creation.py

Status:
    🟡 CONTRACT DESTINATION COLLISION CHECKS ADDED; CATALOGUE RECONCILIATION OPEN

Finding:
    CreationCommandPreparer already rejects duplicate parameter destinations,
    duplicate endpoint destinations, cross-category collisions, and mappings
    that overwrite the object ID. CreationContractRegistry.configure() did not
    apply these checks before accepting a trusted catalogue snapshot, allowing
    invalid mappings to remain configured until a later creation attempt.

Correction:
    Registry configuration now rejects duplicate parameter targets, duplicate
    endpoint targets, parameter/endpoint cross-collisions, and mappings that
    collide with the canonical ID field. This moves these structural failures
    to configuration time while retaining the preparer's defensive checks.

Validation:
    Source change committed as a34d28645f92866f6cd24fe8ada35ab148b6e57a.

GF-MOTOR-023
======================================================================

Files:
    core/application/creation.py

Status:
    🟡 REQUIRED TERMINAL MAPPING INVARIANT ADDED; CATALOGUE RECONCILIATION OPEN

Finding:
    Registry configuration verified that required connection terminals were
    present in the terminal inventory, but did not require each such terminal
    to have an endpoint mapping. A contract could therefore declare a terminal
    mandatory for topology while failing to pass its endpoint to the create
    command.

Correction:
    CreationContractRegistry.configure() now rejects any required connection
    terminal absent from endpoint_mapping. This preserves the separation
    between the terminal inventory and the subset of terminals that must be
    connected at commit, while ensuring every required terminal can be
    represented in the command payload.

Validation:
    Source change committed as 9b4670f9c114f74cd126fa4dc94e8c1bf1a62e31.
    Static source review only; no tests, CI, syntax compilation, or runtime
    imports. All 22 built-in definitions still require complete reconciliation.
    PR #674 remains open, draft, and unmerged.

GF-MOTOR-024
======================================================================

Files:
    core/application/creation_schema.py

Status:
    🟡 MEASUREMENT-TERMINAL ACQUISITION SEMANTICS CORRECTED; STATIC REVIEW ONLY

Finding:
    The built-in CT, PT, and CVT catalogue correctly excluded secondary
    measurement terminals from required electrical topology, but the shared
    terminal helper still marked every physical terminal as required and
    allowed only "electrical" connections. That left S1/S2, secondary_a/b,
    and X1/X2 described as mandatory electrical acquisition terminals in the
    creation schema despite their measurement-channel role.

Correction:
    Added explicit measurement_terminal_names metadata to the terminal helper.
    CT S1/S2, PT secondary_a/secondary_b, and CVT X1/X2 remain in the canonical
    physical terminal inventory but are optional acquisition interfaces and
    are no longer declared as ordinary electrical-wire terminals. Primary
    endpoint mappings and required topology remain unchanged.

    Source-level inspection only. No tests, CI, syntax compilation, or runtime
    imports were run. The Application registry converter currently retains
    terminal names but not the richer terminal acquisition metadata; that
    separate contract-propagation gap remains to be reconciled before startup
    configuration. PR #674 remains open, draft, and unmerged.

    Modification_Register.txt

Status:
    🟡 CANONICAL TERMINAL CONNECTION POLICY ENFORCED; STATIC REVIEW ONLY

Finding:

    physical terminal inventory, but did not enforce the trusted creation
    contract's allowed_connection_types. CT/PT/CVT measurement-secondary
    terminals could therefore be connected through ordinary Simple Wire
    topology despite being declared acquisition interfaces.

    Simple Wire against the trusted canonical terminal requirement before
    DraftNetwork validation and before any Core mutation. Unknown or mismatched
    draft terminal identities and terminals without an electrical/simple_wire
    allowance are rejected with a connection-specific diagnostic. Bus
    attachment endpoints remain governed by the existing Bus endpoint contract.

GF-MOTOR-028
======================================================================

Files:
    plugins/motor_dynamics/tools.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Status:
    🟡 LEGACY WIRE-INSERTION IMPORT AND MOTOR-TO-WIRE BEHAVIOUR REMOVED

Finding:
    MotorPlacementTool imported CommitMotorToNetworkCommand, which is not
    defined/exported by the motor command module, and used a wire hit-test to
    request insertion into an existing branch. That conflicts with the canonical
    one-terminal motor injection model and would fail import when the tool module
    was loaded.

Correction:
    The tool now imports the defined AddDraftMotorPlacementCommand and
    ConnectMotorToBusCommand, optionally resolves a Bus attachment through
    find_bus_attachment_at, and never requests wire splitting/insertion. Invalid
    Bus hit-test results are rejected before creating the motor draft. Placement
    without a Bus hit remains a draft for subsequent editing/connection.
    A failed placement result is returned without dispatching the follow-up Bus
    connection command.

GF-MOTOR-029
======================================================================

Files:
    plugins/motor_dynamics/npu_inference.py
    plugins/motor_dynamics/manifest.json
    Modification_Register.txt

Status:
    🟡 PROVIDER FALLBACK AND INPUT VALIDATION HARDENED; HARDWARE EXECUTION UNVERIFIED

Finding:
    The inference adapter only considered Vitis AI and CPU, did not retry after
    provider initialization failure, and silently truncated or zero-padded input
    features to fit a model. The manifest claimed INT8 support without an
    explicit model-specific quantization contract.

Correction:
    Provider selection now considers Vitis AI, TensorRT, CUDA, and CPU in
    priority order, requires CPU fallback availability, and records provider
    initialization diagnostics. Inference input is checked for finite numeric
    values, exact sample dimensions, and supported floating-point tensor types;
    integer inputs are rejected unless a future explicit quantization contract
    is implemented. Manifest metadata now describes provider capability and
    compatibility limits instead of claiming unverified INT8 support.

Validation:
    Source updates committed as fed22649252e5b8ebd09a507059185c19a3c371f,
    174e6886a7825ca0340b539855fddf58cb85003a, and
    8516439c217bbb8f2dacd3195571818aaf4d9422. Complex-valued features are
    also rejected so their imaginary components cannot be silently discarded. Static review only; no
    tests, CI, syntax compilation, accelerator runtime, or hardware checks were
    run. This does not constitute a complete CPU/GPU/NPU motor simulation solver.

======================================================================

Files:
    core/application/application.py
    core/application/bootstrap.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Status:
    🟡 RUNTIME-SAFE EXTENSION HANDLER FACTORY CONTRACT ADDED; MOTOR SERVICE INTEGRATION OPEN

Finding:
    Application project activation replaces CommandManager instances. Extension
    handlers registered only on the current manager would be lost, while handlers
    closing over project-bound services could remain bound to a stale Network.

Correction:
    Application now exposes register_extension_handler_factory() and
    unregister_extension_handler_factory(). Factories produce command handlers
    against the current/candidate CommandManager. Runtime replacement materializes
    and validates all extension handler mappings before swapping active Application
    references; collisions or factory errors abort the candidate replacement.
    Registration and unregistration update the active registry atomically and
    preserve core ownership of command execution and project mutation.

Validation:
    Application source updates committed as 9eea4b9bf00d779baf017db289003c58911e587d
    and 06194f919d9a30b7a8a0f9c0b0cbbd1f4057fd8c (candidate setup completes
    before the active runtime references are swapped).
    Bootstrap now snapshots and restores extension-handler ownership metadata on
    both activation failure and explicit lifecycle rollback; update committed as
    d0816a1c17de50c4d01e1f067e237a1b8ea963f7.
    Static source review only. No tests, CI, syntax compilation, import checks, or
    runtime activation were run. The motor-specific host composition adapter and
    canonical DraftNetwork motor service remain absent. PR #674 remains open,
    draft, and unmerged.

GF-MOTOR-032
======================================================================

Files:
    core/network/electrical_boundary.py
    core/application/draft/network.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Status:
    🟡 SOURCE-LEVEL POLICY RECONCILIATION APPLIED; RUNTIME UNVERIFIED

Finding:
    DraftNetwork.validate_connection_candidate() checked endpoint identity,
    role membership, and duplicate pairs, but did not invoke the canonical
    Core EndpointCompatibility policy. The Core policy also returned early
    for Bus pairs before rejecting measurement-secondary terminal domains.
    Consequently, a CT/PT/CVT secondary endpoint could pass draft preflight
    when connected directly to a Bus, despite the recorded GF-MOTOR-026

      secondary endpoint before its Bus fast path.
    - DraftNetwork candidate validation converts both canonical draft
      endpoints to Core EndpointReference values and invokes the same Core
      compatibility policy before authoring mutation.
    - Draft authoring continues to own no electrical compatibility rules;
      it delegates to Core and raises a contextual ValueError for the caller.
    - No alternate topology source or UI-derived electrical truth was added.

Validation boundary:
    Source inspection and targeted replacement checks only. No tests, CI,
    syntax compilation, import checks, runtime activation, or hardware checks
    were run. Confirm behavior through the existing SimpleWire service and
    Core endpoint policy during a separately requested verification pass.

GF-MOTOR-033
======================================================================

Files:
    core/application/creation_schema.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Status:
    🟡 SOURCE-LEVEL VALIDATION CONSISTENCY CORRECTED; RUNTIME UNVERIFIED

Finding:
    The framework-neutral EngineeringParameterDefinition validator accepted
    non-finite float values because NaN comparisons do not trigger minimum or
    maximum checks. Its integer conversion path could also raise inconsistent
    conversion errors for non-finite values. Unsupported datatypes fell through
    without an explicit rejection. The Application commit validator separately
    performed stricter finite-number and datatype checks, creating inconsistent
    draft/pre-commit feedback.

Correction:
    - Shared schema validation now rejects NaN and positive/negative infinity.
    - Integer validation checks finiteness and integral value before conversion.
    - Unknown parameter datatypes fail explicitly.
    - Application commit validation remains authoritative; this aligns earlier
      schema feedback with its finite-number and datatype contract.

Validation boundary:
    Source replacement and repository re-fetch only. No tests, CI, syntax
    compilation, import checks, or runtime validation were run.

Disposition:
    Proposed on feature/motor-dynamics-extension-v2 in draft PR #674.
    PR remains unmerged.

GF-MOTOR-034
======================================================================

Files:
    core/application/creation_schema.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Status:
    🟡 NORMALIZED-VALUE VALIDATION RULES ALIGNED; STATIC SOURCE REVIEW ONLY

Finding:
    The neutral creation-schema validator converted numeric parameters for
    datatype and range checks, but then applied nonzero and allowed_values
    rules to the original input. Numeric strings such as "0" could therefore
    bypass nonzero checks, and equivalent numeric inputs could receive
    different allowed_values results than the Application commit validator.

Correction:
    The schema validator now applies non_empty, nonzero, and allowed_values
    rules to the datatype-normalized value, matching the Application
    pre-commit validator's normalization contract without changing Core
    authority or placement-time behavior.

Validation boundary:
    Exact source-block replacement and GitHub source update only. No tests,
    CI, syntax compilation, import checks, or runtime validation were run.

    Proposed on feature/motor-dynamics-extension-v2 in draft PR #674.

    🟡 UNIT-METADATA CONTRACT ALIGNED; STATIC SOURCE REVIEW ONLY

Finding:
    The neutral schema validator accepted unit metadata when the canonical

GF-MOTOR-036

Files:
    core/network/topology.py
    core/network/electrical_boundary.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Status:
    🟡 CT PRIMARY TOPOLOGY PATH RECONCILED; STATIC SOURCE REVIEW ONLY

Finding:
    CurrentTransformer was included in registered physical attachments but
    excluded from TopologyManager's conductive equipment set. A CT primary
    connected between two distinct Buses could therefore be represented as
    two terminal attachments without the zero-impedance series path that
    its primary conductor requires. The secondary measurement circuit remains

      projection; out-of-service CTs do not bridge their attached Buses.
    - Kept CT secondary rejection in EndpointCompatibility, ahead of the
      Bus endpoint fast path. No CT secondary wire or measurement circuit
      was added to the power-network graph.

GF-MOTOR-037
======================================================================

Files:
    core/network/topology.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Status:
    🟡 INSTRUMENT-TRANSFORMER TERMINAL DOMAINS RECONCILED; STATIC SOURCE REVIEW ONLY

Finding:
    PT/CVT secondary terminals could be projected as ordinary physical Bus
    attachments when a Bus was assigned directly, even though ordinary
    Simple Wire validation rejects measurement-secondary terminals.
    The preceding CT correction also treated a four-terminal CT as a
    two-terminal conductive element; that could incorrectly require its
    measurement secondary to resolve as power topology.

Correction:
    - Exclude measurement-secondary terminals from power-network Bus
      attachments and reject direct Bus attachment on those terminals.
    - Keep PT/CVT primary terminals as measurement-device attachments, not
      ideal conductive bridges between their primary terminals.
    - Remove CT from the generic two-terminal topology-element set.
    - Resolve only CT P1/P2 as the dedicated primary series path; require
      both primary terminals to resolve for an in-service CT and add the
      bus adjacency without projecting S1/S2 into power topology.
    - Reuse EndpointCompatibility.domain as the canonical terminal-domain
      classifier.

Validation boundary:
    GitHub source updates and source-level re-fetch only. No tests, CI,
    syntax compilation, import checks, or runtime topology rebuild were run.
    The topology behavior and exact terminal-role contracts remain
    runtime-unverified.

Disposition:
    Proposed on feature/motor-dynamics-extension-v2 in draft PR #674.
    PR remains unmerged.

GF-MOTOR-038
======================================================================

Files:
    core/model/relay.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Status:
    🟡 RELAY MEASUREMENT-CHANNEL BINDING CONTRACT TIGHTENED;
        STATIC SOURCE REVIEW ONLY

Finding:
    Relay.bind_input() validated that a channel had an 'id' attribute,
    but did not verify that the channel exposed a supported live-value
    interface. Invalid objects could be accepted as relay inputs and fail
    later when a protection function attempted to read a measurement.

Correction:
    - Require a non-empty string channel identity.
    - Require either a 'value' attribute or callable get_value() method,
      matching the interfaces consumed by Relay.get_input_value().
    - Keep the Relay independent of a concrete MeasurementChannel class
      and do not copy/cache measurement values or derive them from SLD data.

Validation boundary:
    Source update and targeted source re-fetch only. No tests, CI, syntax
    compilation, import checks, or runtime protection/measurement flow were
    run. The concrete measurement-channel implementation and end-to-end
    CT/PT/CVT-to-relay integration remain to be audited.

    Proposed on feature/motor-dynamics-extension-v2 in draft PR #674.
    PR remains unmerged.

Status:
    🟡 CT/PT TERMINAL-DOMAIN CLASSIFICATION ALIGNED WITH CANONICAL TYPES;
        STATIC SOURCE REVIEW ONLY

Finding:
    EndpointCompatibility._DOMAIN_ROLES keyed CT/PT domains by Python-style
    names ('current_transformer' and 'potential_transformer'), while the
    canonical EquipmentType values derive from model TYPE and are 'ct' and
    'pt'. As a result, CT S1/S2 and PT secondary terminals could fall back
    to the generic domain, bypassing secondary-domain rejection and
    allowing incorrect power-network attachment classification.

Correction:
    - Added canonical 'ct' and 'pt' keys to the domain-role map.
    - Retained descriptive legacy aliases for compatibility.
    - CT/PT secondary roles now resolve through the same classifier used by
      ordinary Simple Wire validation and topology attachment filtering.
    - No measurement values or topology are inferred from SLD graphics.

Validation boundary:
    Source update and targeted source re-fetch only. No tests, CI, syntax
    compilation, import checks, or runtime topology rebuild were run.
    CT/PT/CVT-to-measurement-channel and relay integration remains
    runtime-unverified.

Disposition:
    Proposed on feature/motor-dynamics-extension-v2 in draft PR #674.
    PR remains unmerged.

GF-MOTOR-040
======================================================================

Files:
    core/measurement/measurement_generation.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Status:
    🟡 MEASUREMENT SOURCE FAMILY AND PRIMARY TERMINAL CONTRACT TIGHTENED;
        STATIC SOURCE REVIEW ONLY

Finding:
    MeasurementGeneration validated source identity and that the supplied
    terminal role existed on the source, but it did not enforce the
    instrument family or primary-side role required by the conversion.
    A CT secondary terminal (S1/S2), PT secondary terminal, or CVT
    secondary terminal could therefore be supplied as the source terminal
    while the implementation converted a primary-side network quantity
    through the instrument ratio.

Correction:
    - Current and short-circuit generation now require a CT source.
    - Voltage generation now requires a PT or CVT source.
    - The source terminal must be an authoritative primary-side terminal:
      CT P1/P2, PT primary_a/primary_b, or CVT H1/H2.
    - Preserve explicit source identity and terminal membership checks.
    - Keep analysis values sourced from prepared solver/result contracts;
      no SLD graphics or UI state is consulted.

Validation boundary:
    Source update and targeted source re-fetch only. No tests, CI, syntax
    compilation, import checks, or runtime measurement generation were run.
    Measurement provisioning, channel persistence, and relay consumption
    still require end-to-end integration audit.

Disposition:
    Proposed on feature/motor-dynamics-extension-v2 in draft PR #674.
    PR remains unmerged.

GF-MOTOR-041
======================================================================

Files:
    core/model/relay.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Status:
    🟡 RELAY CONSUMPTION ALIGNED WITH ENGINEERING SIGNAL SEMANTICS;
        STATIC SOURCE REVIEW ONLY

Finding:
    MeasurementChannel.value is explicitly a compatibility alias for
    raw_value. The channel's consumer-facing engineering signal is exposed
    through signal/engineering_value, which applies scale and polarity.
    Relay.get_input_value() preferred value and therefore returned the raw
    sample, silently bypassing channel-level scale/polarity. It also did
    not reject a channel whose is_usable state explicitly indicated invalid,
    unavailable, unknown-quality, or stale data.

Correction:
    - Prefer signal, then engineering_value, before legacy get_value/value
      interfaces so canonical MeasurementChannel consumers receive the
      engineering-scaled and polarity-adjusted value.
    - If the channel exposes is_usable, require it to evaluate to True
      before returning a value.
    - Preserve compatibility for generic channel adapters without the
      optional usability interface.

Validation boundary:
    Source update and targeted source re-fetch only. No tests, CI, syntax
    compilation, import checks, or runtime relay/protection execution were
    run. End-to-end measurement generation and protection consumption remain
    runtime-unverified.

Disposition:
    Proposed on feature/motor-dynamics-extension-v2 in draft PR #674.
    PR remains unmerged.

GF-MOTOR-042
======================================================================

Files:
    core/protection/preparation.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Status:
    🟡 DETACHED PROTECTION SNAPSHOT PRESERVES MEASUREMENT USABILITY;
        SOURCE-LEVEL CORRECTION ONLY

Finding:
    ProtectionPreparation detached channel values into PreparedMeasurementValue
    with availability and quality only. It omitted the canonical channel's
    is_usable/validity state, so offline protection consumers could receive a
    numerical engineering value without an explicit indication that the
    measurement was invalid, unavailable, unknown-quality, or otherwise unusable.

Correction:
    - Snapshot the channel's basic is_usable state and validity classification
      alongside the engineering value.
    - Keep MeasurementChannel authoritative; the preparation layer records a
      detached view and does not mutate the channel or infer protection logic.
    - Staleness remains time-dependent and is not evaluated here because this
      preparation API has no evaluation-time argument; that requires a separate
      execution-time contract rather than guessing a clock.

Validation boundary:
    Source update and targeted source re-fetch only. No tests, CI, syntax
    compilation, import checks, or offline protection runtime were run.

    Proposed on feature/motor-dynamics-extension-v2 in draft PR #674.

GF-MOTOR-043
======================================================================

Files:
    core/persistence/project_persistence.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Status:
    🟡 PERSISTED PROTECTION INPUT REFERENCES VALIDATED;
        SOURCE-LEVEL CORRECTION ONLY

Finding:
    Project persistence validated the shape of measurement definitions but did
    not cross-check ProtectionFunctionConfiguration.input_channel_ids against
    the persisted channel definitions at load/save boundaries. A protection
    input could therefore refer to a channel absent from the project package,
    leaving a dangling binding until a later activation or execution path.
    Duplicate persisted channel IDs were also not rejected at this boundary.

Correction:
    - Validate that each persisted channel ID is a non-empty string and unique.
    - Validate every persisted protection input channel ID against the project's
      measurement channel ID set during both load and save validation.
    - Fail with ProjectPersistenceError and include the protection element/input
      context rather than silently retaining a dangling association.
    - Keep live MeasurementChannel instances and sample state under the
      Application MeasurementChannelService; persistence checks configuration
      identity only.

Validation boundary:
    Source update and targeted source re-fetch only. No tests, CI, syntax
    compilation, import checks, or project round-trip runtime were run.

    Proposed on feature/motor-dynamics-extension-v2 in draft PR #674.

    Modification_Register.txt

Status:
    🟡 PROTECTION MEASUREMENT REFERENCES REQUIRE CANONICAL IDENTIFIERS;
        SOURCE-LEVEL CORRECTION ONLY

Finding:
    The persistence boundary checked that protection input channel references
    existed, but did not first validate their type and canonical form.
    Malformed persisted mappings could therefore trigger an incidental
    TypeError during set membership (for example, a list-valued channel ID),
    or permit empty/whitespace-only input names to pass the boundary.

    - Require each channel reference to be a non-empty string with no
      surrounding whitespace before checking membership against persisted
      channel IDs.
    - Preserve the existing missing-reference rejection and duplicate
      persisted-channel-ID validation.

GF-MOTOR-045
======================================================================

Files:
    core/protection/project_configuration.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Status:
    🟡 PERSISTED PROTECTION ENABLE/BLOCK FLAGS REQUIRE STRICT BOOLEAN TYPES;
        SOURCE-LEVEL CORRECTION ONLY

Finding:
    ProtectionFunctionConfiguration.from_dict() used bool(value) coercion for
    enabled and blocked. Non-empty strings such as "false" and "0" therefore
    became True, while malformed persisted configuration could silently change
    whether a protection function was enabled or blocked.

Correction:
    - Require enabled and blocked to be actual booleans when deserializing.
    - Preserve defaults for omitted fields (enabled=True, blocked=False).
    - Reject malformed persisted values instead of guessing intent.

Validation boundary:
    Source update and targeted source re-fetch only. No tests, CI, syntax
    compilation, import checks, or protection runtime were run.

GF-MOTOR-046
======================================================================

Files:
    core/protection/project_configuration.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Status:
    🟡 PROTECTION CONFIGURATION DESERIALIZATION NO LONGER COERCES IDENTITY
    OR PRIORITY TYPES; SOURCE-LEVEL CORRECTION ONLY

Finding:
    GF-MOTOR-045 corrected boolean coercion on persisted enable/block flags.
    Follow-up audit found the same trust-boundary issue for element_id,
    relay_id, function_code, and priority: from_dict() stringified identities
    and int()-coerced priority. This could turn null identities into the
    literal string "None" or truncate non-integral numeric priority values.

Correction:
    - Pass persisted identity values to the canonical constructor without
      string coercion, allowing its non-empty-string contract to reject
      malformed types.
    - Pass priority without int() coercion so the existing strict integer
      contract rejects booleans, strings, and non-integer values.
    - Enforce enabled/blocked Boolean types in __post_init__ as well as
      from_dict, covering direct construction and deserialization paths.

Validation boundary:
    Source update and targeted source re-fetch only. No tests, CI, syntax
    compilation, import checks, or protection runtime were run.

    Proposed on feature/motor-dynamics-extension-v2 in draft PR #674.

    Modification_Register.txt

Status:
    🟡 ANSI 50/51 BOOLEAN-TO-NUMERIC COERCION REJECTED;
        SOURCE-LEVEL CORRECTION ONLY

Finding:
    ANSI 50 and 51 settings converted pickup and time-multiplier inputs
    using float() before checking their numeric contract. Python booleans
    are numeric subclasses, so True could be accepted as 1.0 for a pickup
    or TMS value and silently alter protection behaviour.

    - Reject Boolean TMS values for ANSI 51.
    - Preserve existing finite-positive numeric validation and IEC curve
      normalization.

GF-MOTOR-064
======================================================================

Files:
    core/application/application.py
    core/application/services/motor_dynamics_service.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    MotorDynamicsComposition returned an idempotent local teardown callback,
    but Application.unregister_extension_handler_factory() removed whichever
    registration currently occupied the plugin ID. If an older teardown
    callback survived after its registration was removed and the same ID was
    registered again, invoking the stale callback could remove the newer
    registration. The previous comment claiming stale callbacks were safe was
    not enforced by the Application API.

      and leaves the active registration untouched when the owner differs.
    - MotorDynamicsComposition retains the exact factory object it registered
      and supplies it as the expected owner during teardown.
    - Existing callers that omit expected_factory retain the prior API
      behavior; the motor lifecycle now uses ownership-checked teardown.

    checks, or application runtime were performed.

GF-MOTOR-066
======================================================================

Files:
    core/application/application.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    Application._replace_runtime() rolled back handlers only within the
    extension currently being registered. If a later extension factory,
    handler registration, or control-runtime setup failed, handlers from
    earlier extensions and SLD registration could remain on the candidate
    CommandManager even though the Application retained its previous runtime.

Correction:
    - Track each SLD and extension handler successfully installed on the
      candidate manager across the entire runtime composition.
    - On any candidate setup failure, unregister those newly installed
      handlers in reverse order on a best-effort basis, preserving the
      original setup exception.
    - Keep publication of the new Application runtime references after all
      candidate composition and control setup has succeeded.

    Targeted source re-fetch only. No tests, CI, syntax compilation, import
    checks, or runtime activation were performed.

GF-MOTOR-067
======================================================================

Files:
    core/application/application.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    Application._replace_runtime() assumed its CommandManager argument was
    a separate candidate. It did not enforce that precondition. If passed
    the active manager, partial SLD-handler registration could mutate the
    currently active registry before a later duplicate-handler collision
    raised, undermining candidate rollback isolation.

Correction:
    - Reject runtime replacement when the supplied CommandManager is the
      active manager, before registering any SLD or extension handlers.

GF-MOTOR-080

Files:
    core/application/application.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:

    installed by each extension. If a handler was removed and another handler

      replacement or missing handlers untouched and retaining metadata for

      only after the complete candidate runtime composition succeeds.

GF-MOTOR-081
======================================================================

Files:
    plugins/motor_dynamics/plugin.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    MotorDynamicsPlugin.shutdown() discarded all teardown callbacks even when
    one failed, cleared the dashboard reference even when close() failed, and
    reset application/context state unconditionally. A failed resource release
    could therefore become impossible to retry through the plugin lifecycle.

Correction:
    - Retain only teardown callbacks that raised; successful callbacks are not
      repeated on a subsequent shutdown attempt.
    - Retain the dashboard reference when close() fails and clear it only after
      close() returns successfully.
    - Preserve initialized state and application/context/persistence references
      whenever resource teardown fails, allowing diagnosis and retry.
    - Clear lifecycle references only after all owned resources are released.

Validation:
    Targeted source and register re-fetch only. No tests, CI, syntax compilation,
    import checks, or plugin runtime activation/shutdown were performed.

    Corrected in draft PR #674; unmerged.

GF-MOTOR-082
======================================================================

Files:
    plugins/motor_dynamics/plugin.py
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    MotorDynamicsPlugin.initialize() swallowed dashboard-close and command
    teardown failures during initialization rollback. The plugin then re-raised
    only the setup exception without retaining failed resource handles, making
    partially registered handlers or a dashboard impossible to retry through
    shutdown(). Shutdown also used ExceptionGroup despite collecting
    BaseException instances, which could mask the original cleanup failure.

Correction:
    - Attempt dashboard close and command teardown independently after setup
      failure, recording each cleanup failure instead of suppressing it.
    - If rollback completes, re-raise the original initialization exception
      without publishing partially initialized plugin state.
    - If rollback is incomplete, retain only failed resource handles and the
      context/application/persistence references needed for retry; mark the
      plugin initialized so shutdown() can retry cleanup.
    - Aggregate initialization and cleanup failures with BaseExceptionGroup.
      Use BaseExceptionGroup during shutdown as well, so captured
      KeyboardInterrupt/SystemExit subclasses do not cause a secondary
      grouping TypeError that masks cleanup failures.

Validation:
    Targeted source and register re-fetch only. No tests, CI, syntax compilation,
    import checks, or plugin runtime activation/shutdown were performed.

Disposition:
    Corrected in draft PR #674; unmerged.

GF-MOTOR-083
======================================================================

Files:
    plugins/motor_dynamics/transient_solver.py
    plugins/motor_dynamics/__init__.py
    plugins/motor_dynamics/manifest.json
    plugins/motor_dynamics/README.md
    Modification_Register.txt

Finding:
    The motor extension exposed telemetry/read-model scaffolding but lacked a
    numerical transient solver. Optional ONNX Runtime accelerator providers
    did not provide motor-physics execution and could be mistaken for GPU/NPU
    solver support.

Correction:
    - Added a headless NumPy CPU reference solver for a balanced squirrel-cage
      induction motor in a synchronous dq frame, integrating stator/rotor flux
      linkages, rotor speed, and a lumped winding-temperature state with
      fixed-step classical RK4.
    - Added SI parameter and time/profile validation, inductance-matrix
      nonsingularity checks, finite-state failure detection, and a uniform
      integration grid ending exactly at the requested simulation duration.
    - Stores trajectory state in one contiguous flat motor-major,
      step-major, state-minor buffer and exposes read-only state/channel views.
    - Added an optional Numba-CUDA batch kernel mapping independent motor
      trajectories to GPU threads. CPU remains the reference/fallback; CUDA
      unavailability or execution failure falls back to CPU with diagnostics.
    - Added result-to-read-model publication support. The application study
      orchestrator remains responsible for publishing only successful results
      and emitting the canonical StudyCompleted event.
    - Exported the solver API, declared its capabilities in the plugin
      manifest, and documented the model boundary and acceleration policy.
    - Explicitly recorded that NPU acceleration is not currently applicable to
      this recurrent integration kernel; ONNX NPU providers remain separate
      inference capabilities and are not represented as physics acceleration.

Model boundary:
    Balanced induction motor; synchronous dq frame; linear inductances;
    constant load torque; one lumped thermal state. Magnetic saturation,
    unbalanced faults, PWM switching, separate stator/rotor thermal nodes,
    load torque curves, and validated reference cases remain outstanding.

Validation:
    Targeted source/document/register re-fetch only. No tests, CI, syntax
    compilation, imports, runtime activation, numerical benchmark, CPU/GPU
    equivalence run, or hardware validation was performed.

Disposition:
    Implemented in draft PR #674; unmerged. Numerical validation and
    application study-lifecycle integration remain required before production
    use.

    trying to report the original lifecycle failure.

    - On Python 3.10, raises a RuntimeError with all failure summaries and
      retains the original exception objects in an errors attribute.

GF-MOTOR-086
======================================================================

Scope:
    Application StudyService integration for motor transient dynamics.

Files:
    core/application/study.py
    plugins/motor_dynamics/study_handler.py
    plugins/motor_dynamics/plugin.py

Finding:
    The transient solver and dashboard existed, but no lifecycle-owned
    motor_transient handler connected validated StudyRequest inputs to
    solver execution and successful StudyCompleted publication.

Correction:
    Added MotorTransientStudyHandler. It accepts explicit MotorParameters
    (or a field mapping), simulation interval, dq voltage inputs, frequency,
    load torque, and backend selection. Constant dq inputs use the solver's
    CPU/CUDA dispatch with CPU fallback; sampled voltage profiles use the CPU
    reference integrator. NPU physics execution is not claimed because no
    applicable motor-physics NPU backend is implemented.

    Added MotorTransientResultPublisher. It retrieves the canonical stored
    StudyResult after StudyService has recorded completion and publishes only
    when status is completed and study_type is motor_transient. Failed and
    cancelled studies do not publish read-model snapshots.

    Added identity-checked StudyService.unregister(...) so plugin teardown
    cannot remove a replacement handler. Plugin lifecycle registers the study
    handler and result publisher, unwinds them on initialization failure, and
    retains teardown handles if cleanup fails. Publisher subscribes before the
    dashboard so its read-model snapshot is available to the dashboard event.

Status:
    SOURCE CORRECTION COMMITTED — STATIC RE-FETCH VERIFIED.

Verification boundary:
    No tests, CI, syntax compilation, imports, runtime execution, numerical
    validation, GPU hardware validation, or NPU validation were performed.
    The solver remains an engineering model whose fidelity limitations are
    documented in the solver source.

GF-MOTOR-090

Scope:
    Align feature-branch StudyService teardown with the shared Application
    lifecycle contract.

File:
    core/application/study.py

Finding:
    The feature branch implemented handler-identity-safe unregister(), but
    could unregister a study type while one of its handlers was executing.
    This allowed plugin teardown to race an active study and diverged from
    the canonical lifecycle correction proposed in PR #676.

    - Refuse to unregister a study type while that type has active work.

      remove a replacement registration.
    - Increment active counts atomically with handler lookup/token creation
      and decrement in the execution finally path.

GF-MOTOR-091 — Extension handler ownership rollback integrity
- Scope: `core/application/bootstrap.py` project-runtime activation and compensation.
- Correction: snapshot and restore `_extension_handler_handlers` alongside `_extension_handler_command_types` when candidate runtime activation fails or is rolled back. This keeps exact handler-identity ownership metadata aligned with the restored CommandManager and prevents stale/mismatched extension teardown records.
- Validation: static source correction only; no tests, CI, syntax/import checks, runtime activation, or hardware validation performed.


GF-MOTOR-092 — Retryable motor extension teardown
- Scope: `core/application/services/motor_dynamics_service.py`, `MotorDynamicsComposition.register_motor_extension()` teardown callback.
- Finding: the callback marked itself unregistered before calling Application teardown. If teardown raised before removing the registration, the callback became permanently non-retryable while ownership could remain installed.
- Correction: set the local `registered` flag to false only after `unregister_extension_handler_factory()` returns. Exceptions preserve retryability; a normal false return still closes the stale callback because it no longer owns the registration.
- Validation: source-level correction only; no tests, CI, syntax/import checks, runtime lifecycle checks, or hardware validation performed.


GF-MOTOR-093 — Motor solver capability and result-lifecycle documentation reconciliation
- Scope: `plugins/motor_dynamics/README.md`.
- Finding: the README simultaneously described the transient solver as absent and documented its RK4 implementation; it also described publication before `StudyCompleted`, contrary to the implemented StudyService subscriber flow.
- Correction: describe the existing solver as preliminary and unvalidated, distinguish its implemented CPU/RK4 path from the limited optional CUDA batch path and inference-only NPU providers, and document that the result publisher verifies the stored successful `StudyResult` after `StudyCompleted` before publishing read-model channels.
- Validation: documentation/source reconciliation only; no tests, CI, numerical validation, syntax/import checks, runtime lifecycle checks, or hardware validation performed.


GF-MOTOR-094 — CUDA RK4 local-array dtype name correction
- Scope: `plugins/motor_dynamics/transient_solver.py`, nested Numba CUDA `integrate` kernel.
- Finding: five CUDA local arrays referenced `dtype=float64`, but the module did not define or import the bare name `float64`. CUDA kernel compilation could therefore fail before the RK4 integration executes.
- Correction: use the already-used `np.float64` dtype explicitly for all five local arrays, matching the kernel's state-array dtype contract.
- Validation: static source correction and re-fetch only; no tests, Numba compilation, syntax/import checks, numerical comparison, CUDA execution, CI, or hardware validation performed.

GF-MOTOR-079
======================================================================

Scope:
    Shared headless ODE integration foundation

Files:
    core/numerics/ode.py

Status:
    🟡 IMPLEMENTED — CONSUMER INTEGRATION AND VALIDATION PENDING

Finding:
    Motor-dynamics integration had a plugin-local CPU RK4 loop. Repeating
    that loop in each future physics plugin would duplicate numerical
    integration behavior and error handling.

Correction:
    Added a domain-neutral, headless RK4 primitive with a uniform time-grid
    contract, explicit zero-order-hold interval inputs, shape and finiteness
    validation, a contiguous flat state-history buffer, and read-only result
    views. The utility imports no Qt, plugin, or accelerator runtime.

Boundary:
    Physics plugins continue to own their derivative equations, parameters,
    state definitions, and domain outputs. CPU is the reference integrator.
    GPU/NPU dispatch remains a separate backend capability and must not be
    inferred from this CPU primitive.

Remaining:
    Refactor the motor CPU solver to consume this shared primitive, reconcile
    CPU/GPU numerical behavior, define backend capability/diagnostic contracts,
    and validate the implementation. No tests or runtime checks were run.

GF-MOTOR-081
======================================================================

Scope:
    Motor batch input validation before backend dispatch

Files:
    plugins/motor_dynamics/transient_solver.py

Status:
    🟡 IMPLEMENTED — STATIC REVIEW ONLY

Finding:
    Batch input validation previously checked shapes and model object types,
    but finite voltage, finite/non-negative electrical frequency, and finite
    numeric load torque were not all rejected before CUDA selection. Invalid
    input could therefore enter the accelerator path before the CPU fallback
    reported the input problem.

Correction:
    Added backend-independent validation for scalar dq voltage inputs,
    electrical frequency, and each load torque before CPU/CUDA dispatch.
    Invalid user input now fails at the batch API boundary rather than being
    treated as an accelerator failure.

Remaining:
    Validate error consistency and CPU/CUDA numerical equivalence when
    runtime validation is authorized and suitable hardware is available.
    No tests, syntax checks, imports, hardware checks, or CI were run.

GF-MOTOR-083
======================================================================

Scope:
    CPU/CUDA model parity review and NumPy scalar input validation

Files:
    plugins/motor_dynamics/transient_solver.py

Status:
    🟡 IMPLEMENTED — RUNTIME VALIDATION PENDING

Audit finding:
    Static comparison found the CPU and CUDA kernels use matching state order,
    inductance/current reconstruction, temperature-adjusted resistance,
    electromagnetic torque, copper-loss calculation, dq flux derivatives,
    mechanical equation, thermal equation, initial speed/temperature, RK4
    stages, uniform time grid, and flat step-major state layout. This is a
    source-level comparison only; numerical parity is not established.

Confirmed defect:
    Public scalar validation accepted Python int/float but rejected some
    NumPy integer/floating scalar values, even though these are ordinary
    numerical inputs commonly produced by NumPy-based callers. The check was
    inconsistent across parameter, time, frequency, torque, and batch paths.

Correction:
    Added a shared numeric-scalar predicate that accepts Python and NumPy
    integer/floating scalars while rejecting Python and NumPy booleans and
    non-scalar values. Applied it across motor parameter, time, frequency,
    torque, and batch validation. Positive-integer pole-pair validation also
    accepts NumPy integer scalars while rejecting booleans.

Boundary:
    This fixes scalar type compatibility and documents the static parity
    findings. It does not prove CPU/CUDA equivalence, convergence, or physical
    model validity. The CPU and CUDA derivative equations remain separately
    implemented because the CUDA kernel must be device-compilable.

Validation:
    Source-level changes only. No tests, syntax checks, imports, CUDA/NPU
    hardware execution, or CI were run.

Files:

Status:

    core.numerics.ode.uniform_time_grid via the plugin's _validate_time adapter.
    Removed the now-unused duplicate ceil dependency from the motor plugin.

Boundary:
    This centralizes the CPU/CUDA time-grid contract and input type handling.
    It does not make the CUDA kernel use the shared CPU RK4 implementation;
    the CUDA kernel retains device-compilable RK4 mechanics. Numerical parity
    and convergence remain unverified.

GF-MOTOR-085
======================================================================

Scope:
    Backend-neutral numerical capability and dispatch policy

Files:
    core/numerics/backend.py
    core/numerics/__init__.py

Status:
    🟡 IMPLEMENTED — CONSUMER INTEGRATION AND VALIDATION PENDING

Finding:
    The architecture had backend-specific decisions embedded in individual
    plugin dispatch code. Future numerical plugins risk duplicating workload
    suitability, precision support, availability, preference, and fallback
    policy.

Correction:
    Added BackendCapability, BackendSelection, and select_backend() as
    runtime-neutral numerical policy primitives. Runtime adapters supply
    availability and supported-workload facts; the policy evaluates workload,
    precision, minimum workload size, explicit requests, automatic preference,
    CPU fallback, and structured rejection reasons. Exported these primitives
    from core.numerics.

Boundary:
    The shared policy does not probe hardware or import CPU/GPU/NPU runtimes.
    It does not claim NPU support or dispatch existing motor work until the
    motor plugin is explicitly integrated. Application/study orchestration
    remains responsible for supplying truthful runtime capability facts and
    publishing successful results.

    Source-level implementation only. No tests, syntax checks, imports,
    hardware probing, or CI were run. Consumer integration remains pending.

GF-MOTOR-087 — Reusable numerical-plugin problem contract
Files: core/numerics/ode.py; core/numerics/__init__.py
Status: Implemented; validation pending.
Added frozen NumericalProblem with problem identity, workload key, plugin derivative, initial state, duration, maximum step, optional interval input sampler, and float64 precision declaration. Added solve_numerical_problem() to route plugin-defined ODEs through shared CPU RK4. Physics equations remain plugin-owned; accelerator adapters remain separate and must prove workload/precision compatibility. This does not yet migrate the motor solver to the new contract or provide generic GPU/NPU execution. No tests, syntax checks, imports, hardware execution, or CI were run.

GF-MOTOR-088 — Motor solver adopts shared NumericalProblem contract; numerical backend adapter Protocol added to core.numerics.backend and exported. CPU motor equations now populate the common problem envelope and call solve_numerical_problem(), preserving plugin-owned derivative/state definitions. The adapter contract requires compatible state ordering, time-grid, precision, and input-sampling semantics; runtime adapters supply capability facts and own runtime-specific failures. GPU adapter conformance and actual NPU runtime are not implemented by this change. No tests, syntax checks, imports, hardware execution, numerical comparison, or CI were run.

GF-MOTOR-089 — Motor CUDA batch adapter integration. Added a motor-specific CUDA batch adapter whose capability declaration explicitly limits execution to motor_transient_batch, float64, and batches of at least eight independent motors. Shared dispatch selects from the adapter-provided capability and execution now passes through its solve_batch entry point. CPU and CUDA result diagnostics now identify workload and precision consistently; both paths use the shared uniform time-grid contract. CUDA exceptions still trigger diagnosed CPU fallback. This is source-level only: no numerical equivalence, syntax, import, test, CUDA hardware, performance, or CI checks were run. The generic NumericalBackendAdapter protocol remains distinct from this motor-specific batch adapter because the current CUDA kernel cannot execute arbitrary plugin derivative callables.

GF-MOTOR-090 — CPU/CUDA transient parity audit and fail-fast correction. Source comparison confirmed the CUDA derivative equations match the CPU motor derivative for the supported constant-dq workload, including temperature-adjusted resistance, slip, torque, copper loss, mechanical acceleration, and lumped thermal dynamics. Both paths use the shared uniform grid rule (ceil(duration/max_step), exact duration endpoint) and initialize flux states to zero with configured speed/temperature. Corrected one identified failure-contract discrepancy: CUDA now writes a non-finite sentinel and exits when an accepted RK4 state becomes non-finite; the host finite-state check raises into the existing diagnosed CPU fallback. This improves divergence handling but does not prove bitwise or tolerance-based numerical equivalence. No tests, syntax checks, imports, numerical comparison, CUDA hardware execution, performance measurement, or CI were run.

GF-MOTOR-091 — RK4 intermediate-stage failure contract aligned. The shared CPU reference solver now rejects a non-finite intermediate stage state before invoking the derivative, in addition to its existing derivative-output and accepted-state checks. The CUDA motor batch kernel now checks each RK4 derivative vector and constructed intermediate stage, then marks the motor output as failed so the host finite-state guard enters the existing diagnosed CPU fallback. This narrows the CPU/GPU failure-contract gap; it does not establish identical exception timing or numerical equivalence. No tests, syntax checks, imports, numerical comparisons, CUDA hardware execution, performance measurement, or CI were run.

GF-MOTOR-092
======================================================================

Scope:
    CUDA motor-batch failure attribution and batch-result integrity

Files:
    plugins/motor_dynamics/transient_solver.py

Status:
    🟡 IMPLEMENTED — SOURCE REVIEW PENDING; RUNTIME VALIDATION DEFERRED

Finding:
    The CUDA kernel marked only one output scalar as non-finite when a motor
    integration stage failed. The host-wide finite-state check prevented that
    partial batch from being returned, but the reported failure did not
    identify the affected motor or RK4 stage.

Correction:
    Added a per-motor integer status buffer. The CUDA kernel records the first
    failed RK4 derivative/intermediate/accepted-state stage for that motor.
    The host copies and inspects status before constructing any result objects;
    if any motor failed, it discards the entire accelerator batch and raises a
    diagnostic identifying the first failing batch index and stage code. The
    existing non-finite output guard remains as a secondary integrity check.
    The existing batch dispatcher records the exception and retries the
    complete batch through the CPU reference solver.

Stage codes:
    1 k1 derivative; 2 k2 input state; 3 k2 derivative;
    4 k3 input state; 5 k3 derivative; 6 k4 input state;
    7 k4 derivative; 8 accepted RK4 state.

Boundary:
    A stage code is diagnostic metadata, not a proof of physical divergence
    or backend parity. A failed GPU batch is never returned as partially
    successful results. CPU fallback can still fail independently and then
    propagates that CPU failure.

Validation:
    Source edits only. No tests, syntax checks, imports, Numba compilation,
    CUDA hardware execution, numerical comparisons, performance measurements,
    or CI were run.
