GF-ROUTING-ORTHOGONAL-001 — Issue #817 — Obstacle-Aware Orthogonal Routing
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    ui/connections/connection_router.py; Issue #817.

Changes:
    1. Replaced the direct-only route implementation with a deterministic
       rectilinear visibility-grid router.
    2. Added finite-coordinate and axis-aligned bounds validation, obstacle
       clearance, bend cost, and penalties for intersections with existing
       routes.
    3. Added path invariants: no duplicate consecutive points, no diagonal
       segments, and no silent straight-line fallback when obstacles block
       every route.
    4. Kept routing renderer-neutral. The router does not resolve terminals,
       change electrical connectivity, or mutate Core state.

Integration boundary:
    SLDCanvasRenderSystem supplies scene-space bounds for realized
    non-endpoint nodes and realized connection polylines when it calculates
    automatic routes. The router excludes source/target node bounds from its
    obstacle set and uses existing routes as soft crossing/overlap penalties.
    These are presentation inputs only; the router does not infer or mutate
    electrical connectivity. Runtime near-miss verification and explicit
    route-edit interaction review remain follow-up work before Issue #817 can
    be closed.

Verification:
    Repository source change only. No tests or CI were run, per repository
    rules. Python syntax/import integrity and runtime route cases remain to be
    checked before merge.

Status:
    IMPLEMENTED ON fix/issue-817-orthogonal-routing — PENDING REVIEW AND
    SOURCE-LEVEL VERIFICATION. Issue #817 remains open.


======================================================================
GF-ROUTING-ORTHOGONAL-002 — Issue #817 — Exterior Visibility Corridor
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    ui/connections/connection_router.py; fix/issue-817-orthogonal-routing.

Finding:
    The visibility grid initially used only source/target coordinates and
    obstacle clearance edges. When obstacle clearance regions overlap, those
    coordinates can leave no usable path around the outside of the obstacle
    group even where an exterior orthogonal route is geometrically possible.

Correction:
    Added deterministic exterior corridor coordinates beyond the full
    obstacle envelope, with a margin derived from the obstacle span and
    configured clearance. The router can now consider paths around the outside
    of overlapping obstacle groups without changing endpoint identities or
    electrical topology.

Verification:
    Source correction committed to the feature branch. No tests or CI run.
    Python syntax/import integrity and runtime route scenarios remain pending
    under the repository's stated acceptance process.

Status:
    EXTERIOR VISIBILITY CORRIDOR CORRECTED — ISSUE #817 REMAINS OPEN. Existing
    route crossing integration and the near-miss electrical-connectivity path
    still require direct source audit and verification.

GF-ROUTING-ORTHOGONAL-003 — Issue #817 — Existing Route Awareness
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    ui/canvas/sld_canvas_render_system.py; ui/connections/connection_router.py.

Finding:
    The renderer passed obstacle bounds to the router but did not pass existing
    realized connection polylines. The router's crossing penalty capability was
    therefore unused by the active SLD auto-route path.

Correction:
    The renderer now collects route endpoints and intermediate route points
    from already-realized connection items and supplies those polylines as
    soft crossing-penalty inputs for new automatic routes. Unsupported/stale
    visual geometry is skipped rather than allowed to affect electrical state.
    Existing paths remain presentation-only; this does not mutate routes or
    establish electrical connectivity.

Verification:
    Source correction committed to the feature branch. No tests or CI run.
    Python syntax/import integrity and runtime routing scenarios remain pending.

Status:
    EXISTING ROUTE PENALTIES WIRED INTO RENDERER — ISSUE #817 REMAINS OPEN.
    The separate near-miss connectivity symptom still requires tracing through
    the active authoring and Application command path before closure.

GF-ROUTING-ORTHOGONAL-004 — Snap Candidate Lifecycle Cleanup
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    ui/core/snap_system.py; fix/issue-817-orthogonal-routing.

Finding:
    SnapSystem.dispose() cleared the candidate and callback registries without
    disconnecting movement signals registered on graphics items. A long-lived
    item could retain a callback to the disposed service and later attempt a
    refresh against an inactive SnapSystem.

Correction:
    Disposal now disconnects each registered movement callback before clearing
    candidate references. Already-deleted Qt senders and already-disconnected
    signals are handled as cleanup no-ops. Core topology and snap identity are
    unchanged.

Verification:
    Source correction committed to the feature branch. No tests or CI run.
    Syntax/import integrity and Qt lifecycle behavior remain pending review.

Status:
    SNAP CANDIDATE SIGNAL CLEANUP CORRECTED. ISSUE #817 REMAINS OPEN WHILE THE
    NEAR-MISS VISUAL-VERSUS-ELECTRICAL CONNECTIVITY PATH IS STILL UNVERIFIED.

GF-ROUTING-ORTHOGONAL-006 — Existing Route Coordinate Space
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    ui/canvas/sld_canvas_render_system.py; fix/issue-817-orthogonal-routing.

Finding:
    SLDConnectionItem.visual_endpoints() and route_points() expose coordinates
    from the item's local QGraphics path, while ConnectionRouter receives
    scene-space source/target coordinates. Passing local existing-route points
    directly can skew crossing penalties when an item has a non-identity scene
    transform.

Correction:
    Existing connection endpoints and bends are now mapped through mapToScene
    before being supplied to the router. Items without that mapping capability
    retain their exposed coordinates as a compatibility fallback; unsupported
    geometry remains non-authoritative presentation data.

Verification:
    Source updated on the feature branch. No tests or CI run. Syntax/import and
    transformed-item runtime behavior remain pending source-level verification.

Status:
    EXISTING ROUTE COORDINATE SPACE NORMALIZED. ISSUE #817 REMAINS OPEN.

GF-ROUTING-ORTHOGONAL-013 — Undo Failure and Pre-Commit Rollback Audit
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    core/application/command_manager.py; core/application/transaction.py;
    fix/issue-817-orthogonal-routing.

Audit:
    1. CommandManager invokes the Application pre-commit hook while the
       transaction remains open. Handler, result-validation, and hook failures
       enter the same rollback boundary.
    2. Transaction.rollback() attempts all registered inverse operations in
       reverse order and records ROLLBACK_FAILED if any inverse fails. Rollback
       finalizers run only if every content inverse succeeds.
    3. CommandManager._rollback_safely() marks the manager DEGRADED when rollback
       fails, preventing subsequent commands from proceeding on uncertain state.
    4. A failed undo marks the manager DEGRADED; if zero inverse operations are
       reported complete, it attempts to restore the undo history record first.
       The fail-closed decision remains necessary because an inverse may mutate
       before raising.
    5. Post-commit history-recording failures also mark the manager DEGRADED;
       committed Core state is not incorrectly rolled back.

Correction decision:
    No additional code change was warranted by this source pass. The inspected
    transaction and history paths already implement reverse-order rollback,
    continue attempting remaining inverses after an inverse failure, and fail
    closed when Core/history integrity is uncertain.

Verification:
    Source inspection only. No tests/CI, syntax/import checks, failure injection,
    or runtime transaction/undo verification were performed.

Status:
    SOURCE-LEVEL FAILURE-PATH AUDIT RECORDED. Issue #817 remains open pending
    the required end-to-end SLD/network runtime verification.

GF-RECONCILE-2026-10-29-G — Protection/control concurrency contracts
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    Issues #839, #840, #845, #859.

Source findings:
    - #839: MeasurementChannel accepts finite scalar and complex values and carries
      phase, signal type, scale/polarity, quality, and sample metadata. TopologyManager
      excludes measurement-secondary terminals from conductive Bus topology. These
      boundaries exist, but a complete primary-to-instrument-transformer-to-channel-
      to-protection phase-isolation/vector contract is not established by this audit.
    - #840: ControlInterlock and ControlEngine provide logic-level interlock gates.
      Application.execute serializes each command through an RLock, but this does
      not itself establish a multi-command physical interlocking contract across
      multiple targets. No parallel Pre-Execution Lock Registry should be added
      without a demonstrated race and approved command precondition semantics.
    - #845: CloseBreakerCommand exists; no synchronism-check evaluator or explicit
      ANSI 25 voltage/frequency/phase tolerance contract was found in reviewed
      Application entrypoints. A safe correction requires a defined measurement
      contract and fail-closed rules; do not infer synchronism from topology alone.
    - #859: TopologyManager builds island/topology snapshots and tracks topology
      revision; it does not own global frequency state or a CUDA frequency buffer.
      The alleged race owner/backend is not established by current source evidence.

Disposition:
    - Keep all four issues open pending the specific source/runtime evidence and
      contracts recorded in their issue comments.
    - Any ANSI 25 implementation must execute at the Application/Core breaker-close
      mutation boundary and record its decision; it must not be a UI-only warning.
    - Measurement secondary references remain outside conductive power topology.
    - Frequency is study/runtime state, not TopologyManager-owned topology state.
    - No implementation or issue closure is claimed by this audit.

Verification boundary:
    Source inspection only. No tests/CI, syntax/import checks, concurrent runtime
    trace, solver/device profiling, or protection study was run.

======================================================================
GF-RECONCILE-2026-10-29-H — Measurement, transient, and protection model gaps
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    Issues #841, #842, #843, #846, #847, #848, #849, #851, #852, #853,
    #854, #855, #856, #857.

Findings and dispositions:
    - #841: Dynamics package exposes event, DAE, and transient-stability solvers.
      The post-protection-trip topology-change reinitialization contract remains
      unproven. Require a trip-to-solver event trace and explicit post-event
      initialization/failure semantics.
    - #842: MeasurementChannel documentation assigns saturation, filtering, and
      instrument error to the physical measurement source/model. CT/PT saturation
      must be implemented as a physical instrument model with validated excitation,
      remanent-flux, burden, and secondary waveform contracts—not generic clipping.
    - #843: Anti-aliasing belongs to the acquisition/measurement-generation boundary
      with explicit sample rate and filter response; do not silently filter the
      generic logical MeasurementChannel.
    - #846: The claimed SER single-array bottleneck is unproven. Require the actual
      recorder, append path, buffer policy, timestamp owner, and profile.
    - #847: Multi-relay bus-zone trip requires an explicit atomic multi-target
      Application command if partial clearing is reproducible. Individual command
      serialization is not a multi-command atomicity guarantee.
    - #848: Frequency/ROCOF estimator and derivative spikes are not established by
      the issue narrative alone. Require the active estimator, sample interval,
      waveform, and response-time/error budget.
    - #849: Identify the exact interlock override owner and project.json path before
      changing persistence. Overrides must be explicit and auditable project state,
      not hidden untyped runtime booleans.
    - #851: Require the canonical ANSI 21 zone implementation, settings, input
      snapshot provenance, and out-of-zone fault reproduction before changing
      protection-zone coordination.
    - #852: Point-on-wave switching requires a defined sampled-waveform source,
      zero-crossing interpolation, noise/DC-offset model, and breaker delay contract.
    - #853: Application serializes individual commands; an atomic UFLS/UVLS batch
      contract and actual concurrent dispatch path have not been established.
      Any batch must be Application-owned and rollback-capable.
    - #854: A canonical persisted relay setting-group model and dynamic switch path
      were not established. Future changes must publish immutable setting snapshots
      at an evaluation boundary.
    - #855: Require a traced ANSI 50BF timer event → typed target mapping →
      Application command path; protection worker threads must not mutate Core
      directly.
    - #856: Cold-load pickup desensitization is a scoped load/reconnection transient
      model and coordination contract, not a global threshold increase.
    - #857: DC-offset and fundamental phasor treatment must follow the specific
      protection element's measurement quantity and an identified acquisition
      contract; do not generically replace peak/instantaneous values with filtered
      values.

Disposition:
    Keep all scoped issues open pending the source/runtime evidence and domain
    contracts recorded in the issue threads. No new protection thresholds, solver
    workarounds, filtering behavior, or unverified physics were introduced.

Verification boundary:
    Source and issue inspection only. No tests/CI, syntax/import checks, transient
    simulation, relay coordination study, event-buffer profiling, or runtime fault
    injection was performed.

GF-RECONCILE-2026-10-29-I — New authoring, playback, merge, and plugin capabilities
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Scope:
    Issues #826, #827, #830, #833, #834, #835, #836.

Findings and dispositions:
    - #826: Tool/CreationContext state is transient; DraftNetwork authoring state is
      Application-owned. A dedicated draft undo journal is not established by the
      committed command history. Define gesture cancellation, draft undo/redo, and
      commit semantics before changing Escape behavior.
    - #827: Inline insertion already has an explicit SelectTool/ElectricalInsertionTool
      path. Proximity assist may select a candidate, but cannot infer electrical
      insertion intent from distance alone. Keep PT/CVT out of series insertion
      without their own electrical contracts.
    - #830: Obstacle-aware orthogonal routing is on main through PR #837 and overlaps
      #817. This issue's distinct increment is live trajectory preview; reuse the
      canonical router rather than create a second A* implementation.
    - #833: Time-indexed dynamic-study playback is a new presentation capability.
      Define StudyResult time-series schema, deterministic cursor, pause/seek,
      scheduling/backpressure, and read-only projection semantics.
    - #834: The 2D canvas needs a profiled spatial-index/culling design, not a 3D
      octree by default. Destructive scene unloading can invalidate selection,
      snap registration, and object lifecycle; preserve canonical document state.
    - #835: Visual three-way project merge needs stable identities, schema migration,
      field-level conflict classification, and typed Application patch commands.
      Generic “blend” behavior is unsafe for electrical parameters without
      property-specific semantics.
    - #836: Process-isolated plugin execution is the appropriate direction for
      untrusted third-party code, but requires versioned IPC, explicit capabilities,
      resource/time limits, cancellation/termination, and an SDK migration path.
      Current in-process Python plugins are not a sandbox.

Disposition:
    Keep all scoped issues open as capability/contract work. No feature was marked
    implemented merely because the architecture direction was described. Do not
    pass Qt objects, Application instances, live Core objects, or arbitrary Python
    callables across a future plugin process boundary.

Verification boundary:
    Source and issue inspection only. No tests/CI, syntax/import checks, profiling,
    multi-process plugin exercise, playback runtime, or merge-conflict fixture was run.

======================================================================
2026-10-29 — WORKSPACE TRANSITION ROLLBACK RECOVERY CORRECTION
======================================================================

Scope
-----
ui/workspace/project_workspace.py
ProjectWorkspaceLifecycle._restore_transition_snapshot()

Confirmed defect
----------------
Rollback previously stopped immediately when WorkspaceController.deactivate()
raised. That prevented independent restoration attempts for the project identity,
document collection, active document, view collection, active view, and prior
workspace/layout. A realization-layer failure could therefore suppress recovery
of logical workspace state and obscure additional recovery failures.

Correction applied
------------------
- Rollback now attempts workspace deactivation, document/view clearing,
  project restoration, each document/view registration, active selections,
  and workspace/layout restoration as independent steps.
- A failed step no longer prevents later independent restoration attempts.
- All restoration failures are retained in a combined diagnostic; the first
  failure is preserved as the exception cause and individual failures are
  attached as exception notes when supported.
- The frozen boundary remains unchanged: this is UI workspace lifecycle
  recovery; it does not move project/domain truth into UI or introduce Qt
  dependencies into Core.

Branch
------
fix/workspace-transition-rollback-recovery

Commit
------
bdb47b0794d9f40ad3667145b724098fe0d53570

Verification status
-------------------
Source-level replacement was applied against the current main version and
committed to the focused correction branch. No tests, CI, GUI runtime, or
import checks were run. Project-scoped undo/redo history and cross-workspace
selection/action synchronization remain open audit items pending their own
source trace; they are not marked corrected by this entry.

======================================================================
WORKSPACE SWITCH SELECTION / ENGINEERING CONTEXT CORRECTION
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Branch:
    fix/workspace-transition-rollback-recovery

Files:
    main.py

Finding:
    The canonical workspace activation path changed the active workspace
    and updated only EngineeringContext.discipline. The shared SLD
    SelectionManager retained selected IDs, and EngineeringContext could
    retain the previous active_tool and selected_ids. Editor-context
    construction reads the shared selection, so IDs from the prior surface
    could leak into a newly activated workspace.

Correction:
    After successful WorkspaceController.activate(), clear the shared UI
    selection and publish the new discipline, that surface's active tool,
    and an empty selected_ids tuple to EngineeringContextStore. A failed
    workspace activation does not clear selection or publish the new context.

Architecture:
    UI-only interaction/presentation state. No Qt or SLD object is passed
    into Core, and no electrical/domain truth is moved out of Core.

Verification:
    Source-level call-path audit only. No tests, CI, GUI runtime, Python
    syntax compilation, or import checks were run. Confirm the
    active_tool_id_for(surface_id) contract and runtime action-state
    behavior during the remaining integration review.

======================================================================
COMPATIBILITY SURFACE SELECTION ISOLATION
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Branch:
    fix/workspace-transition-rollback-recovery

Files:
    main.py

Finding:
    Topology and Map activation uses the compatibility-surface path
    outside WorkspaceController. That path changed discipline and cleared
    active_tool, but retained shared SLD selection and EngineeringContext
    selected_ids. This allowed SLD selection state to remain visible in
    context while a non-SLD surface was active.

Correction:
    After successful compatibility-surface activation, clear the shared
    UI selection and publish the surface discipline, active_tool=None,
    and selected_ids=(). Failed activation does not mutate selection or
    publish the new engineering context.

Architecture:
    UI-only transient interaction/presentation state. Core remains the
    authority for electrical/domain truth; no Qt/SLD objects cross into Core.

Verification:
    Source-level call-path audit only. No tests, CI, GUI runtime, Python
    syntax compilation, or import checks were run.

======================================================================
ACTIVE-DISCIPLINE GUARD FOR SLD SELECTION AND TOOL EVENTS
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-29

Branch:
    fix/workspace-transition-rollback-recovery

Files:
    main.py

Finding:
    The composition root forwarded every SelectionManager selection event
    and every Controller tool_changed event directly into the shared
    EngineeringContextStore. Both sources represent SLD-only state. If
    a hidden SLD canvas emitted a late selection/tool event while Control,
    Protection, Study, Topology, or Map was active, it could overwrite the
    active editor's selected_ids or active_tool context.

Correction:
    Route these signals through named handlers. Selection changes update
    EngineeringContext and the workspace selection context only while the
    active discipline is SLD. Controller tool changes update active_tool
    only while SLD is active; tool shelves are still refreshed for UI
    synchronization.

Architecture:
    Presentation event routing only. No domain truth moved into UI and no
    Qt/SLD objects cross the Core boundary.

Verification:
    Source-level audit and GitHub content update only. No tests, CI, GUI
    runtime, Python syntax compilation, or import checks were run.

2026-10-29 — WORKSPACE ROLLBACK EXCEPTION BOUNDARY CORRECTION
======================================================================

Scope:
    ui/workspace/project_workspace.py
    ProjectWorkspaceLifecycle._restore_transition_snapshot()

Finding:
    The best-effort rollback helper caught BaseException, which also captures
    process-control exceptions such as KeyboardInterrupt and SystemExit and
    converts them into ordinary restoration diagnostics.

Correction:
    Catch Exception for ordinary restoration failures. Process-control
    exceptions are no longer intentionally swallowed or normalized by the
    rollback helper.

Architecture:
    UI lifecycle recovery only. No Core/domain ownership changes.

Verification boundary:
    Source edit committed to fix/workspace-transition-rollback-recovery.
    No tests/CI, syntax/import checks, or GUI runtime were run.

======================================================================
2026-10-30 — WORKSPACE EDITOR-CONTEXT SELECTION ISOLATION CORRECTION
======================================================================

Scope:
    main.py
    _editor_context_factory()

Finding:
    Editor-context construction read selected IDs directly from the shared
    SLD SelectionManager for every editor discipline. The event-routing
    guards added to the workspace activation path did not protect this
    independent read path. A Control, Protection, Study, Topology, or Map
    editor context could therefore observe IDs belonging to a hidden SLD
    canvas.

Correction:
    Read shared SelectionManager IDs only when editor_type is "sld".
    Supply an empty selected_ids tuple to all non-SLD editor contexts.
    The same discipline-scoped tuple is used by EngineeringContext and
    EditorContext.selection_context, preventing divergent selection views.

Architecture:
    UI-only selection projection. The shared SelectionManager remains the
    SLD selection authority; non-SLD contexts receive no SLD selection.
    No Core/domain authority or mutation boundary changed.

Verification:
    Static source inspection confirms the read is guarded by editor_type.
    Python syntax/import and workflow verification are pending after this
    branch update. No tests or CI were run by this correction pass.

GF-PROT-IDMT-TCC-001 — IEC IDMT coordination and TCC workspace
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-30

Issue:
    #868

Scope:
    - Existing IEC 60255 inverse-time equations and IEC SI/VI/EI curve
      constants in core.protection.relay_functions were audited and retained.
    - Existing generate_tcc_points() remains the canonical numerical TCC
      point generator; no duplicate formula engine was introduced.
    - Reused the existing core.protection.coordination.RelayCoordination
      engine for primary/backup and multi-point CTI evaluation; no parallel
      coordination engine or duplicate policy was retained.
    - Reconciled legacy TCCCurve.calculate_time() to delegate to the shared
      relay_functions IEC equation, retaining its legacy curve names and
      zero-TMS compatibility behaviour.
    - Added immutable IDMTCurvePointReadModel / IDMTCurveReadModel,
      generated from persisted ANSI 51/51N settings.
    - Added Application.read_idmt_curves() as the UI-neutral read boundary.
    - Added the Protection TCC Coordination default panel with logarithmic
      current/time axes and multi-curve overlays. Refresh reads snapshots
      through Application; the chart does not calculate protection or
      mutate settings/network state.

Engineering boundaries:
    - Current values retain the relay-input engineering convention; no
      unsupported primary/secondary CT conversion or unit assumption is made.
    - Primary/backup roles, fault-current scenarios, and required grading
      margin must be explicitly supplied by the caller.
    - Backup non-pickup is INDETERMINATE, not a false coordination pass.
    - ANSI 51/51N catalog status is unchanged; chart availability does not
      establish production protection coordination or scheme completeness.
    - The issue's claim that all IDMT mathematics are absent is inaccurate:
      IEC equations, inverse solution, TCC point generation, and primary/backup
      CTI coordination pre-existed. This change consolidates the legacy TCC
      adapter on the canonical equation and adds the missing Application
      read-model and log-log workspace.

Verification:
    - Source changes committed on feature/issue-868-idmt-tcc-workspace.
    - No tests or CI run, per repository rule.
    - Python syntax/import, PySide6 runtime chart rendering, curve overlay
      interaction, and relay-coordination engineering validation remain
      unverified and are not claimed complete.

Disposition:
    - Keep issue #868 open pending source/runtime verification and review of
      project-level primary/backup relationship configuration and scenario
      provenance. No production-ready claim.

GF-PROT-IDMT-WORKFLOW-002 — Application coordination Study Case workflow
======================================================================

Date:
    2026-10-30

Issue:
    #868

Scope:
    - Added immutable CoordinationStudyPair, CoordinationRelayCurrent,
      CoordinationFaultScenario, CoordinationStudyConfiguration,
      CoordinationStudyPointResult, and CoordinationStudyResult contracts
      in core/analysis. Each scenario maps current values and units by
      protection-function identity, so multi-pair studies do not reuse one
      pair's currents for another pair.
    - Added RelayCoordination.check_pair_currents() so primary and backup
      protection functions are evaluated at their independently supplied
      relay-input currents. The existing common-current check_pair() API
      delegates to this method and retains its prior call contract.
    - ShortCircuitInput now carries optional explicit base_mva and per-bus
      nominal voltage bases. ShortCircuitBranchCurrent retains its per-unit
      current and carries from/to side ampere bases when the required source
      data is available. ShortCircuitAnalysis propagates configured base_mva.
    - CoordinationFaultScenario can consume a completed Short-Circuit Study
      result by study ID. The Application resolves only current, completed
      same-project StudyResultReadModel values; the Core verifies result
      project/generation/topology provenance and complete branch coverage.
    - CoordinationStudyPair requires explicit primary/backup branch IDs,
      from/to sides, and per-relay scale factors for Short-Circuit sourced
      scenarios. Each branch pu magnitude is converted to side-specific
      primary amperes, then scaled into the relay's declared input unit.
    - TCC workspace can select a completed Short-Circuit Study, map the
      primary/backup branches and sides, provide conversion scales, run the
      coordination Study Case, and display the source Study ID with CTI rows.
    - Corrected required_additional_delay for a backup non-operation result:
      negative-infinite margin now yields infinite required delay rather
      than incorrectly returning zero.
    - Registered protection_coordination with Application StudyService.
      Coordination cases pass the existing project activation, source
      revision, topology snapshot, project validation, cancellation, and
      StudyCompleted/StudyFailed lifecycle.
    - Added Application create_protection_coordination_study_case(),
      read_protection_coordination_results(), and the project-scoped
      execution handler.
    - Execution uses immutable ANSI 51/51N curve read models and the
      canonical RelayCoordination engine. Explicit pair identities and
      per-scenario primary/backup relay-input currents are mandatory.
    - Physical Relay in_service/enabled/blocked state and ProtectionFunction
      enabled/blocked state are checked before calculation.
    - Added current_unit to IECOvercurrentSettings and persisted relay
      function settings. Coordination refuses to run unless each scenario
      unit exactly matches the configured unit for the respective function.
    - Added a UI workflow to select one primary and one backup ANSI 51/51N
      function, choose manual relay currents or a completed Short-Circuit
      Study, map primary/backup branches and sides, provide current scales,
      set CTI, execute a Study Case, and display CTI margins and violations.
    - Study results are read back through Application StudyResultReadModel
      and are rejected for display if stale against active project revision.

Engineering boundaries:
    - Manual scenarios use explicit relay-input current values. Short-Circuit
      sourced scenarios use the canonical per-unit branch-current records,
      their explicit from/to side ampere bases, and engineer-supplied relay
      current scales.
    - Primary/backup relationships, branch mappings, branch sides, and CT
      conversion scales are never inferred from SLD topology or adjacency.
    - If ShortCircuitStudyConfiguration.base_mva or a branch-side nominal
      bus voltage is unavailable, the result carries no ampere base and
      Short-Circuit sourced coordination fails closed. A caller-supplied
      prebuilt SequenceNetwork without matching base_mva provenance also
      disables per-unit to ampere conversion.
    - Coordination Study Case definitions now serialize into canonical
      project.json using a typed protection_coordination payload. Pair
      identities, branch IDs/sides, conversion scales, CTI, scenario IDs,
      manual relay currents/units, fault locations, and Short-Circuit source
      Study IDs are restored into typed immutable configuration objects.
    - Project load rejects coordination scenarios whose referenced
      Short-Circuit Study Case is missing or is not of short_circuit type.
      It also rejects primary/backup function references absent from the
      project's persisted protection configuration or not configured as
      ANSI 51/51N.
    - Persistence reuses the existing Study Case collection and the existing
      transactional project save/load activation path; no parallel registry
      or sidecar file was introduced.
    - The TCC workspace now supports explicit multi-pair authoring: each
      added pair snapshots its own primary/backup identities and either its
      manual relay-input current series/units or Short-Circuit branch IDs,
      sides, and conversion scales. Pairs can be removed before execution.
    - Manual multi-pair scenarios are assembled by scenario index; all pairs
      must provide the same scenario count and fault location. Shared relay
      function identities must have identical current/unit inputs, otherwise
      authoring fails closed. Short-Circuit pairs must reference one completed
      source Study Case in a single coordination Study Case.
    - The workspace refuses execution until at least one pair is explicitly
      added; no implicit or inferred pair mapping is introduced. Result rows
      identify the primary and backup protection-function IDs so multi-pair
      scenario results remain attributable to their evaluated pair.
    - Application Study Case creation now validates every Short-Circuit source
      against a registered Short-Circuit Study Case and a current, completed
      canonical ShortCircuitResult before registration. Manual relay-input
      units are checked against each referenced protection function's declared
      current unit before a runnable Study Case is created.
    - Project persistence applies symmetric coordination-reference validation
      on save and load. It rejects missing protection-function definitions,
      non-ANSI-51/51N references, and missing/non-Short-Circuit source Study
      Cases before writing or activating a project package.
    - Coordination result reads now expose only completed, current typed
      CoordinationStudyResult payloads; failed, cancelled, stale, or malformed
      results are not presented as completed coordination results.
    - The TCC overlay refuses mixed or undeclared current units; curves are
      not placed on a common logarithmic axis unless their unit labels match.

Verification:
    - Source changes committed on feature/issue-868-idmt-tcc-workspace.
    - No tests or CI run, per repository rules.
    - Python syntax/import integrity and PySide6 runtime rendering were not
      executed and are not claimed verified.

Disposition:
    - Implemented manual and Short-Circuit sourced coordination Study Case
      execution with explicit relay/branch mapping and current conversion.
    - Issue #868 remains open for runtime verification, multi-pair UI authoring,
      and end-to-end engineering validation. No production-ready claim.

2026-10-30 — Protection coordination result classification and integrity
-----------------------------------------------------------------------

Files:
    - core/analysis/coordination_study.py
    - Modification_Register.txt

Changes:
    - Distinguish BACKUP_ONLY_OPERATION from NOT_APPLICABLE. A backup
      protection function that operates while the configured primary does
      not operate is now an explicit coordination violation, rather than
      being silently classified as not applicable.
    - Preserve NOT_APPLICABLE only when neither primary nor backup operates.
    - Include BACKUP_ONLY_OPERATION in aggregate VIOLATION classification.
    - Validate each immutable point result's identities, units, current and
      time domains, CTI, additional-delay value, status, and status/time
      consistency.
    - Validate aggregate pair/scenario counts, unique pair/scenario
      identities, non-empty project provenance, and non-negative integer
      activation/model/topology revisions. Reject incomplete or duplicate
      aggregate point sets before publication.

Verification:
    - No tests or CI run, per repository rules.
    - Python syntax/import integrity and UI runtime rendering not verified.

2026-10-30 — Persisted Study Case staleness across project activation
---------------------------------------------------------------------

Files:
    - core/application/study.py
    - core/application/application.py
    - core/application/bootstrap.py
    - core/persistence/project_persistence.py
    - Modification_Register.txt

Changes:
    - Added an explicit immutable is_stale state to StudyCaseDefinition.
    - Application.study_cases derives staleness from the current electrical
      model/topology revision before persistence, so a case made stale by
      later edits cannot be silently revalidated on reload.
    - Project persistence writes the stale-state marker alongside the
      original source revision. Legacy packages without the marker restore
      Study Cases as stale because their saved project revision baseline
      cannot be proven.
    - Project activation rebases only non-stale Study Cases to the new
      activation's clean revision baseline. Stale cases retain their stale
      marker and cannot execute until reconfigured or recreated.
    - execute_study_case rejects explicitly stale cases as well as cases
      whose live source revision no longer matches the active project.

Verification:
    - No tests or CI run, per repository rules.
    - Python syntax/import integrity and project save/reopen runtime
      verification remain unverified.

2026-10-30 — Cooperative cancellation through Power Flow and Contingency
------------------------------------------------------------------------

Files:
    - core/solver/power_flow/nr_solver.py
    - core/analysis/power_flow.py
    - core/analysis/contingency.py
    - core/application/bootstrap.py
    - Modification_Register.txt

Changes:
    - Added an optional should_cancel callback to the Newton-Raphson
      numerical solver. Cancellation is checked between iterations and
      before accepting a converged result. A running dense linear solve is
      not forcibly interrupted; its output is discarded at the next
      cancellation checkpoint.
    - Exposed the callback through PowerFlowAnalysis.solve() without
      changing existing callers.
    - Wired the Application StudyCancellationToken into the Power Flow
      handler so cancellation during Newton-Raphson cannot be published as
      a successful Power Flow result.
    - Added cooperative cancellation to ContingencyAnalysis.run(). The
      analysis checks between outage cases and passes the callback into
      each case's Power Flow solver, stopping further outage cases once
      cancellation is requested.
    - Wired the Application token into the Contingency handler. StudyService
      remains the sole owner of terminal cancelled/completed arbitration;
      partial contingency output is discarded when cancellation wins.
    - Short-Circuit already has cancellation checkpoints before preparation,
      before solver invocation, and after solver completion in its
      Application handler. Its dense numerical operation remains
      non-preemptible; cancellation is finalized immediately after it
      returns. Transient Stability retains its integration-level callback.

Verification:
    - No tests or CI run, per repository rules.
    - No Python syntax/import checks or numerical runtime interruption
      experiments were executed.
    - Cancellation responsiveness inside NumPy's active dense linear
      algebra operation remains bounded by that operation's completion.

2026-10-30 — Reject stale study results at terminal publication
---------------------------------------------------------------

Files:
    - core/application/study.py
    - core/application/application.py
    - Modification_Register.txt

Changes:
    - Added an Application publication lock to StudyService terminal
      arbitration. Numerical handlers remain concurrent and do not hold the
      Application mutation lock while calculating.
    - Added a final source-freshness guard after handler completion and
      immediately before result registration and terminal event publication.
      The guard verifies active project identity, activation generation, and
      electrical model/topology revision.
    - The terminal check, result-store update, and StudyCompleted/StudyFailed/
      StudyCancelled event publication are serialized against Application
      command mutations. A mutation that wins first causes the calculated
      value to be discarded and a failed result with failure_reason
      "stale_source" to be stored; StudyCompleted is not emitted.
    - Cancellation retains precedence over stale-source rejection when the
      cancellation token was requested before terminal arbitration.
    - Distinct studies still calculate concurrently from detached immutable
      snapshots; project transitions remain blocked while any study is active.

Verification:
    - No tests or CI run, per repository rules.
    - Python syntax/import integrity and concurrent mutation/publication
      runtime behavior were not executed and remain unverified.

2026-10-30 — Invalidate prior results when a Study Case rerun starts
--------------------------------------------------------------------

Files:
    - core/application/study.py
    - Modification_Register.txt

Changes:
    - StudyService now removes the previously published result for the exact
      project/activation/study identity atomically when a new execution is
      accepted and its cancellation token is registered.
    - A read-model request during a rerun therefore cannot return the prior
      completed result as though it represented the in-progress execution.
    - The new execution publishes its own completed, failed, or cancelled
      terminal result. A rejected duplicate active execution does not clear
      the active run's result state.
